A1 Series First Layer Print Issues - First Layer Too Low, Hotend Moving

This guide explains why the first layer of prints too low on A1 series printers, and how to fix it.

---

## [¶](#applicable-printer-models) Applicable Printer Models

A1 series printer

## [¶](#first-layer-too-low) First Layer Too Low

A good first layer is critical for a successful print. If the first layer fails, the entire print is likely to fail.

In this section, we will discuss the reasons behind a too low first layer during printing and provide solutions.

"First layer too low” means that the distance between the nozzle and the build plate is too close, resulting defects such as "peeling," "bulging," and "wavy lines" on the first layer.

The images below shows what this issue looks like:

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](/n1/troubleshooting/fist-layer-issues/fist_layer_1.jpg) | ![](/n1/troubleshooting/fist-layer-issues/customer_2.jpg) |

## [¶](#when-to-use-this-guide) When to Use This Guide

* During first layer printing, issues such as **"lifting/peeling," "wavy patterns,"** or **raised edges** appear;
* Or the **nozzle is too close to the build plate** — nearly touching or scraping the plate surface — resulting in little to no filament being extruded.

## [¶](#cause) Cause

### [¶](#heatbed-leveling-has-not-been-performed) Heatbed leveling has not been performed

It is crucial to perform heat bed leveling, particularly when changing or replacing the build plate, to ensure accurate leveling for the new print task.
Neglecting this step may result in discrepancies compared to the previous leveling data.

### [¶](#the-position-of-the-hotend-changed) The position of the hotend changed

The position of the hot end changes when printing compared with the bed leveling, and the nozzle is closer to the heat bed resulting in excessive extrusion of the first layer.

## [¶](#solution) Solution

### [¶](#step-1-perform-bed-leveling-before-printing) Step 1. **Perform Bed Leveling Before Printing**

Running bed leveling before printing is recommended — especially after changing or replacing the PEI build plate.

This helps ensure a consistent and accurate first layer.

To enable this, turn on the **Bed Leveling** option in the slicer before starting your print.

![](/n1/troubleshooting/fist-layer-issues/bed_leveling_en.jpg)

### [¶](#step-2-check-the-hotend-and-heater-assembly) Step 2. Check the Hotend and Heater Assembly

If bed leveling has already been performed for the current print and the issue persists, try retightening the hotend heater assembly screws.

1. Check the printer screen and confirm the hotend temperature is below 50°C.
2. Turn off the printer.
3. Remove the front cover.
4. Follow the instructions on the guide sticker to remove the hotend.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](/n1/troubleshooting/fist-layer-issues/cut_filament.jpg) | ![](/n1/troubleshooting/fist-layer-issues/remove_the_hotend.jpg) |

1. Use an H2.0 hex key to remove the 3 screws that secure the heater assembly.
2. Flip the heater assembly over to access the 4 small screws on the back.
3. Use an H1.5 hex key to retighten these screws as shown below.

⚠️ These screws are small — handle them carefully to avoid stripping or losing them.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](/n1/troubleshooting/fist-layer-issues/3_screws.jpg) | ![](/n1/troubleshooting/fist-layer-issues/4_screws.jpg) |

> **Note:**
>
> * The 3 heater assembly mounting screws are **M3×10**.
> * The 4 small rear screws are **M2×6** (with flat washer and spring washer).

### [¶](#step-3-check-the-heater-assembly-buckle) Step 3. Check the Heater Assembly Buckle

Make sure the heater assembly buckle (clip) is properly fastened. Refer to the images below for the correct orientation.

**To verify:** Gently try to wiggle the hotend by hand. There should be no obvious movement or looseness.

⚠️ **Important:** If the heater assembly is not properly secured — or if the clip is damaged — the hotend may shift downward during printing due to extrusion pressure. This can cause the nozzle to compress the first layer too much or even scrape the build plate.

![扣紧卡扣.webp](/a1m/troubleshooting/nozzle-clog/%E6%89%A3%E7%B4%A7%E5%8D%A1%E6%89%A3.webp)

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| **✅ Correct — Buckle properly fastened** | **❌ Incorrect — Buckle not properly fastened** |
| ![](/a1/troubleshooting/nozzle.jpg) | ![](/a1/troubleshooting/nozzle_.jpg) |

**After Reassembly:**

1. Reinstall the hotend and front cover.
2. Power on the printer.
3. Navigate to the **Calibration** menu on the printer screen.
4. Run **Auto Bed Leveling** to recalibrate.

![calibration.jpg](/calibration.jpg)

## [¶](#end-notes) End Notes

> We hope the detailed guide provided has been helpful and informative.
>
> To ensure a safe and effective execution, if you have any concerns or questions about the process described in this article, we recommend submitting a [Technical ticket](https://bambulab.com/en/my/support/tickets?from=5) regarding your issue. Please include a picture or video illustrating the problem, as well as any additional information related to your inquiry.

A1/A1 mini Nozzle/Hotend Unclogging Procedure

This guide shows a few examples of how to remove a clog on the A1/A1 mini nozzle/hotend.

---

Hotend clogs are relatively common for FDM 3D printers and they can occur due to various reasons, exemplified below:

* The filament used has a low glass transition temperature (gets softened when printing in an enclosure) - More information about heat creep [available in this Wiki article](/en/filament-acc/filament/heat-creep).
* The filament has an inconsistent diameter, usually larger than the regular 1.75 mm, getting stuck in the hotend.
* Debris present on the filament or inside the extruder has reached the hotend and clogged the nozzle completely or partially
* Particulates from filament have blocked the nozzle tip of the hotend (common when printing with carbon fiber infused filament, glow in the dark or sparkling filament)
* When very different filament types were used and the previous filament was not purged completely (PLA and PC, ASA and TPU, etc)

## [¶](#how-to-resolve-a-filament-clog) How to resolve a filament clog?

**Simple Clog / Partial Clog**

A simple clog or partial clog is considered when the tip of the hotend is clogged with particulates inside the filament (carbon fiber, sparkling filament, etc). These particulates could be larger than the nozzle hole and will block it from being extruded.

Most of the time, these particulates could either clog the hotend completely, where there is no filament being extruded anymore, or a partial clog generated by debris on the filament which manifests itself by filament not flowing straight from the hotend and curling when extruding manually from the hotend.

When such a clog occurs the model is not printing as expected, with inconsistent extrusion leading to bad print quality.
It can also manifest itself with a badly calibrated K value for [Flow Dynamics](/en/software/bambu-studio/calibration_pa) due to the inconsistent nozzle pressure and flow.

![](/h2/troubleshooting/clogging/partial_clog_issues.jpeg)

The easiest procedure to follow in this case is to try and dislodge the small piece blocking the hotend using the included pin you received with the printer.

### [¶](#tools-needed) Tools needed

* Unclogging Pin Tool
* Plier
* Oven glove
* H1.5 or H2.0 Allen key
* Filament

![](/h2/troubleshooting/clogging/tools_needed.jpg)

### [¶](#notes-before-you-start) Notes before you start

> *Please note that some of the operations within this guide could be hazardous if not performed with care as they involve high nozzle temperatures. Please take all precautions necessary when performing these tasks. For some of these procedures, the machine should be powered off and the nozzle should be completely cooled down for most of these procedures.*
>
> *When it is necessary to hold the hotend in your hand, please wear an oven glove and make sure that the floor or tabletop below the hotend is free of flammable materials or furniture or flooring that can be easily damaged by the heat.*

## [¶](#h-1-manually-extrude-the-filament) **1. Manually extrude the filament**

This is the simplest method to unclog the hotend. Switch to the left nozzle on the screen, increase the hotend temperature slightly higher than the filament's recommended temperature, and then extrude the filament by clicking the loading button on the screen.

### [¶](#step-1-heat-the-hotend) **Step 1. Heat the hotend**

Increase the temperature of the hotend to a temperature slightly higher than that required by the filaments (PLA for example, set the hotend temperature to 250°C);

![](/a1/troubleshooting/nozzle-clog/set_temperature.jpg)

### [¶](#step-2-manually-extrude) Step 2. Manually extrude

Click the loading button on the screen to extrude the filament manually. **If using TPU, the loading button cannot be clicked more than three times. And avoid rapidly clicking the unloading button multiple times to prevent jamming or filament entanglement.**

![](/a1/troubleshooting/nozzle-clog/extrude_filament.jpg)

Observe whether the nozzle is extruding properly. As shown in the picture below, the filament should come out straight and consistently.

![](/a1/troubleshooting/nozzle-clog/flow_good_-_copy.png)

## [¶](#h-2-unclog-with-a-pin-tool) 2. Unclog with a pin tool

It is important to note that this method does not work with nozzles smaller than 0.4mm.

If the filament cannot be extruded from the nozzle, or if the filament extruded from the nozzle does not become newly loaded filament, it will be necessary to use a through-needle to unclog the hotend and manually re-extrude the filament (cycling the unclogging with a pin tool and extruding process).

Heat the hotend to a temperature slightly higher than that required by the filament inside the hotend , following the instructions in Step 1 of the “1. Manually extrude the filament” section above. After reaching the temperature, insert the needle into the nozzle and unclog it up and down several times.

> **Warning!** When using a cleaning needle, sudden release of internal nozzle pressure can cause hot filament to spray unexpectedly. Always wear protective gloves and keep your head clear of the printer chamber during cleaning.

![](/a1m/troubleshooting/nozzle-clog/%E9%93%9C%E9%92%88.webp)

## [¶](#h-3-unclogging-with-a-pin-tool-and-cold-pull) 3 Unclogging with a pin tool and cold pull

> We have provided a complete video tutorial along with written instructions and images. Here is the complete video of unclogging with a pin tool and cold pull:
>
> [](https://public-cdn.bblmw.com/wiki/A1-mini/A1-mini-nozzle-clogging-en.mp4)

### [¶](#step-1preparation-before-starting) Step 1：Preparation before starting

Move the Z-axis to the appropriate height, and also move the tool head to the appropriate height; and remove the front cover of the tool head for the next step

![](/n1/troubleshooting/nozzle-clog/06_a1_mini%E6%AD%A3%E9%9D%A2.png)

### [¶](#step-2heat-the-hotend-and-remove-the-filament) Step 2：Heat the hotend and remove the filament

Heat the hotend to 100°C (PLA for example) and remove the hotend while it is heated (**Please operate with oven glove!**).

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](https://wiki.bambulab.com/a1m/troubleshooting/nozzle-clog/%E7%83%AD%E5%BA%8A%E5%8D%87%E6%B8%A9.webp) | ![](https://wiki.bambulab.com/a1m/troubleshooting/nozzle-clog/%E6%89%8B%E5%A5%97%E5%8F%96%E4%B8%8B.webp) |

Remove the filament at the entrance of the hotend with a plier.

![](/a1m/troubleshooting/nozzle-clog/%E9%92%B3%E5%AD%90%E6%8B%94%E4%B8%8B.webp)

### [¶](#step-3placing-the-hotend-and-reheat-it) Step 3：Placing the hotend and reheat it

Placing the hotend back in, set the hotend temperature to 220°C and load another colour of the same filament；

![](/a1m/troubleshooting/nozzle-clog/%E6%89%8B%E5%A5%97%E8%A3%85%E5%9B%9E.webp)

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](https://wiki.bambulab.com/a1m/troubleshooting/nozzle-clog/%E5%8A%A0%E7%83%AD%E5%96%B7%E5%98%B4.webp) | ![](https://wiki.bambulab.com/a1m/troubleshooting/nozzle-clog/%E9%A2%9C%E8%89%B2-.webp) |

### [¶](#step-4manually-extrude-the-filament-and-cold-pull) Step 4：Manually extrude the filament and cold pull

If the extruded filament from the nozzle becomes completely freshly loaded, the hotend temperature can be lowered to 100°C and cold pull can begin.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](/a1m/troubleshooting/nozzle-clog/%E7%83%AD%E7%AB%AF%E6%B8%A9%E5%BA%A6100.png) | ![](/a1m/troubleshooting/nozzle-clog/%E5%86%B7%E6%8B%94.webp) |

### [¶](#step-5-reconnect-the-ptfe-tube-and-test-extrusion) Step 5. Reconnect the PTFE tube and test extrusion

Reconnect the PTFE tube and load the filament and test the extrusion. If there is still no extrusion or under extrusion, several more attempts to clear the hotend will be required.

## [¶](#h-4-hot-hex-wrench-unclogging-method) 4. Hot hex wrench unclogging method

The images in this section feature the H2 nozzle. Since it is very similar to the A1 nozzle, the procedure is identical.

A common clog that occurs on A1 series is the filament getting stuck inside the hotend due to heat creep issues. This phenomenon was covered in detail [in this article](/en/filament-acc/filament/heat-creep), and we recommend reading it to understand how to avoid this type of clog.

For the next steps, you will need to remove the nozzle. [This wiki page covers the full removal and reinstall of the A1 nozzle, in case you need it.](/en/a1/maintenance/replace-hotend)

> Caution! **We recommend using heat-protective gloves for this operation**. The molten filament can drip on your fingers causing burns.

### [¶](#step-1-heat-up-the-hex-wrench) Step 1. Heat up the hex wrench

Start by **heating a hex wrench** for approximately 10 seconds. Use a set of pliers to hold the hex wrench firmly and prevent burning yourself.

![](/h2/troubleshooting/clogging/heat_up_allen_key.jpg)

### [¶](#step-2-insert-the-hex-wrench-on-the-nozzle-inlet) Step 2. Insert the hex wrench on the nozzle inlet

While the tip of the wrench is hot, quickly push it in the hotend, through the top end of the heatsink.
The hot wrench will go inside the filament, as shown in the image below. Then, wait for about 30 seconds for the hex wrench to get cold.

![](/h2/troubleshooting/clogging/insert_the_hex_wrench.jpg)

### [¶](#step-3-heat-the-tip-of-the-hotend-and-pull-filament-clog) Step 3. Heat the tip of the hotend and pull filament clog

The hex wrench is now stuck inside the hotend, with filament around it. To remove the wrench with the filament clogging the hotend, you will need to heat the tip of the hotend.

First, remove the silicone sock of the hotend, then apply heat using the lighter to the tip of the nozzle for about 20 seconds (when using a regular lighter).

> Caution!: **Avoid heating the nozzle for a longer period or using a high power butane torch.** A regular gas lighter is recommended.
> The hot side of the nozzle needs to only be hot enough so you can remove the hex tool with the filament.
> Overheating the nozzle can lead to the filament dripping or popping from the tip. Carefully follow the instructions and don't point the nozzle tip towards you when doing this procedure.

![](/h2/troubleshooting/clogging/heat_up_hot_end.jpg)

After heating the tip of the nozzle for 20 seconds, you can pull slowly on the wrench. You will notice that the filament comes out from the hotend in a single piece, clearing the clog.

![](/h2/troubleshooting/clogging/pulled_filament.jpg)

### [¶](#step-4-reinstall-the-nozzle) Step 4. Reinstall the nozzle

You are now ready to reinstall the nozzle. [This wiki page covers the full removal and reinstall of the A1 nozzle, in case you need it.](/en/a1/maintenance/replace-hotend)

## [¶](#how-to-verify-completionsuccess) How to verify completion/success

Reinstall the nozzle, connect the PTFE tubes, and reload the filament to test the extrusion. If there is still clogging or insufficient extrusion, it is necessary to repeatedly attempt cleaning the hotend.

### [¶](#confirm-the-nozzle-is-flowing-correctly) Confirm the nozzle is flowing correctly

After moving the pin through the nozzle tip a few times, remove the pin and extrude some filament using the on-screen buttons. The filament should flow in a straight line and should not curl during extrusion, as shown in the image below.

![](/a1/troubleshooting/nozzle-clog/flow_good_-_copy.png)

While the above methods tend to clear out 99% of clogs, there might be cases where a nozzle simply needs to be replaced, as it could be damaged. If after all these, your machine can print normally, then you have successfully unclogged your nozzle.

## [¶](#note) Note

0.2mm nozzles are much more difficult to clear clogs from and might require the above steps several times over. On the other hand, nozzles larger than 0.4mm tend to clog a lot less than 0.4mm ones as the opening of the nozzle allows for larger particles to go through.

## [¶](#end-notes) End notes

> We hope the detailed guide provided has been helpful and informative.
>
> To ensure a safe and effective execution, if you have any concerns or questions about the process described in this article, we recommend submitting a [technical ticket](https://bambulab.com/en/my/support/tickets?from=5) regarding your issue.
>
> Please include a picture or video illustrating the problem, as well as any additional information related to your inquiry.

A1 mini Squeaky Heatbed Repair Guide

This guide will show you how to fix a squeaky heatbed on A1 mini.

---

## [¶](#video-guide) Video Guide

## [¶](#applicable-models-of-printers) Applicable models of printers

A1 mini

## [¶](#when-to-use) When to use?

Moving the heatbed will cause abnormal noises.

## [¶](#tools-and-materials-needed) Tools and materials needed

* cleaning cloth
* H2.0 hex wrench
* cotton swab
* lubricant grease
* lubricant oil

![tools.png](/a1m/troubleshooting/heatbed-repair/tools.png)

> **IMPORTANT!**
>
> It's crucial to **power off the printer before conducting any maintenance work**, including work on the printer's electronics and tool head wires. Performing tasks with the printer on can result in a short circuit, leading to electronic damage and safety hazards.
>
> During maintenance or troubleshooting, you may need to disassemble parts, including the hotend. This exposes wires and electrical components that could short circuit if they contact each other, other metal, or electronic components while the printer is still on. **This can result in damage to the printer's electronics and additional issues.**
>
> Therefore, it's crucial to **turn off the printer and disconnect it from the power source before conducting any maintenance**. This prevents short circuits or damage to the printer's electronics, ensuring safe and effective maintenance. For any concerns or questions about following this guide, we recommend submitting a [technical ticket](https://bambulab.com/en/my/support/tickets?from=5) regarding your issue and we will do our best to respond promptly and provide the assistance you need.

## [¶](#operational-guide) Operational guide

### [¶](#remove-the-y-axis-rear-cover) Remove the Y-axis rear cover

Step 1. Turn off the power.

![关电源.png](/a1m/troubleshooting/heatbed-repair/%E5%85%B3%E7%94%B5%E6%BA%90.png)

Step 2. Move the heatbed forward.

![热床前移.png](/a1m/troubleshooting/heatbed-repair/%E7%83%AD%E5%BA%8A%E5%89%8D%E7%A7%BB.png)

Step 3. Remove the Y-axis rear cover screws.

![拆除螺丝.png](/a1m/troubleshooting/heatbed-repair/%E6%8B%86%E9%99%A4%E8%9E%BA%E4%B8%9D.png)

Step 4. Remove the Y-axis rear cover.

![移除后盖.png](/a1m/troubleshooting/heatbed-repair/%E7%A7%BB%E9%99%A4%E5%90%8E%E7%9B%96.png)

### [¶](#lubricate-the-eddy-current-coil-and-y-axis-belt) Lubricate the eddy current coil and Y-axis belt

Step 1. Remove the eddy current coil screw.

![移除涡流线圈螺丝.png](/a1m/troubleshooting/heatbed-repair/%E7%A7%BB%E9%99%A4%E6%B6%A1%E6%B5%81%E7%BA%BF%E5%9C%88%E8%9E%BA%E4%B8%9D.png)

Step 2. Place the eddy current coil base upwards and wipe it with a cleaning cloth.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](/a1m/troubleshooting/heatbed-repair/%E6%B6%A1%E6%B5%81%E7%BA%BF%E5%9C%88%E5%BA%95%E5%BA%A7%E6%9C%9D%E4%B8%8A.png) | ![](/a1m/troubleshooting/heatbed-repair/%E6%B8%85%E6%B4%81%E5%B8%83.png) |

Step 3. Apply lubricant grease with a cotton swab and apply it evenly on the eddy current coil.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](/a1m/troubleshooting/heatbed-repair/%E6%A3%89%E7%AD%BE%E8%98%B8%E5%8F%96%E6%B6%A6%E6%BB%91%E8%84%82.png) | ![](/a1m/troubleshooting/heatbed-repair/%E5%9D%87%E5%8C%80%E6%B6%82%E6%8A%B9.png) |

Step 4. Apply lubricant oil to the surface of the Y-axis belt.

![涂抹润滑油.png](/a1m/troubleshooting/heatbed-repair/%E6%B6%82%E6%8A%B9%E6%B6%A6%E6%BB%91%E6%B2%B9.png)

Step 5. Press the Y-axis belt tightly with a cleaning cloth and push the heatbed to spread the lubricant oil evenly.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](/a1m/troubleshooting/heatbed-repair/%E7%94%A8%E6%B8%85%E6%B4%81%E5%B8%83%E6%8C%89%E7%B4%A7_y_%E8%BD%B4%E7%9A%AE%E5%B8%A6%EF%BC%8C%E5%B9%B6%E5%89%8D%E5%90%8E%E6%8E%A8%E5%8A%A8%E7%83%AD%E5%BA%8A.png) | ![](/a1m/troubleshooting/heatbed-repair/%E4%BD%BF%E6%B6%A6%E6%BB%91%E6%B2%B9%E5%9D%87%E5%8C%80%E6%B6%82%E6%8A%B9.png) |

### [¶](#install-the-eddy-current-coil-and-y-axis-rear-cover) Install the eddy current coil and Y-axis rear cover

Step 1. Reinstall the eddy current coil and lift it to **avoid scratching the belt**.

![装回涡流线圈.png](/a1m/troubleshooting/heatbed-repair/%E8%A3%85%E5%9B%9E%E6%B6%A1%E6%B5%81%E7%BA%BF%E5%9C%88.png)

Step 2. Install the eddy current coil screw.

![安装涡流线圈螺丝.png](/a1m/troubleshooting/heatbed-repair/%E5%AE%89%E8%A3%85%E6%B6%A1%E6%B5%81%E7%BA%BF%E5%9C%88%E8%9E%BA%E4%B8%9D.png)

Step 3. Install the Y-axis rear cover.

![安装_y_轴后盖.png](/a1m/troubleshooting/heatbed-repair/%E5%AE%89%E8%A3%85_y_%E8%BD%B4%E5%90%8E%E7%9B%96.png)

Step 4. Install the Y-axis rear cover screws.

![安装后盖螺丝.png](/a1m/troubleshooting/heatbed-repair/%E5%AE%89%E8%A3%85%E5%90%8E%E7%9B%96%E8%9E%BA%E4%B8%9D.png)

### [¶](#post-operation-test) Post-operation Test

Move the heatbed back and forth to test whether there is any abnormal noise.

![测试效果.png](/a1m/troubleshooting/heatbed-repair/%E6%B5%8B%E8%AF%95%E6%95%88%E6%9E%9C.png)

## [¶](#end-notes) End Notes

> We hope the detailed guide provided has been helpful and informative.
>
> To ensure a safe and effective execution, if you have any concerns or questions about the process described in this article, we recommend submitting a [technical ticket](https://bambulab.com/en/my/support/tickets?from=5) regarding your issue. Please include a picture or video illustrating the problem, as well as any additional information related to your inquiry.

# A1 mini Bed Tramming | Bambu Lab Wiki

Capturado: 2026-07-18T21:58:12.968267+00:00

---

A1 mini Bed Tramming

This guide describes the process of manually tramming/leveling the bed

---

The heatbed of the Bambu Lab A1 mini has been adjusted before leaving the factory. After completing the operation according to the instructions in the Quick Start, you can print directly. However, if the heatbed is abnormal due to transportation, or the heatbed is disassembled or replaced due to product maintenance, then we need to ensure that the heatbed of the printer is flat through bed tramming.

## [¶](#prepare-for-bed-tramming) Prepare for Bed Tramming

Before starting the bed tramming procedure, it's important to prepare the printer.

1. Remove the hotend sock and ensure that the nozzle is clean and free from any filament on the tip. If needed, heat up the nozzle and wipe the nozzle to ensure it is clean;

![](/n1/bed-leveling/clean_nozzle.jpg)

2. Make sure the 3 heatbed locking screws under the heatbed are fully loosened；

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](/n1/bed-leveling/3_screws.jpeg) | ![](/n1/bed-leveling/loosen.jpg) |

3. Remove the build plate and clean the surface of the heatbed, then tighten the heatbed leveling screw until the bottom end of the screws sit flush with the bottom bracket.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](/a1-mini/manual/update-firmware-from-sd-card/a1mini%E7%83%AD%E5%BA%8A.jpg) | ![](/n1/bed-leveling/level_screw_position2.jpg) |

> Note: Just make sure that the 4 leveling screws are flush with the bottom bracket, avoiding any protrusion of the screws from the bottom bracket or any indentation.

## [¶](#start-the-bed-tramming) Start the Bed Tramming

To begin the bed leveling process, first, download the attached g-code file, transfer it to a MicroSD Card, and then power on the printer.

[a1mini\_manual\_bed\_screws\_adjust\_assist.gcode](/n1/bed-leveling/a1mini_manual_bed_screws_adjust_assist.gcode)

After the printer completes the startup and can successfully identify the file, select the above Gcode file and click to run it, just like you would start a new print.

> **Note:** This G-code file **only supports running from an SD card** and is not compatible with Bambu Studio.

Please note that A1 mini SD card is not hot-swappable. Please turn off the power before inserting or removing the SD card to avoid any potential damage or corruption.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](/n1/bed-leveling/sd_card.jpg) | ![](/n1/bed-leveling/file.jpg) |

After the g-code file is loaded, the printer will home, then move over to the front left corner and wait for 30 seconds. When this happens, carefully turn the corresponding bed leveling screw until the printing surface barely touches the nozzle.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](/n1/bed-leveling/position_1.jpg) | ![](/n1/bed-leveling/screw_leveling.jpg) |

Reminder: Tightening the leveling screws clockwise will increase the distance between the heatbed and the nozzle; conversely, loosening the leveling screws counterclockwise will make the heatbed closer to the nozzle.

**Please make sure that the nozzle is just touching the printing surface**. The nozzle **should not push against the printing surface**, and **there should be no gap between the nozzle and the bed**.

|  |  |  |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ![](/n1/bed-leveling/snipaste_2023-10-17_20-36-33.jpg) | ![](/n1/bed-leveling/correct_en.jpg) | ![](/n1/bed-leveling/tool_high_en.jpg) |

When you find that the nozzle starts to actively lift, be sure to stop the leveling action and remove the hex key because the tool head will start to move.

After 30 seconds, the nozzle will move to the front right corner of the heatbed. Repeat the procedure until the nozzle barely touches the bed. The same will happen on the rear part of the bed.

When the first leveling is done, the process will be repeated two more times, to allow you to fine-tune the adjustment and double-check the distance between the nozzle and the bed. The entire Gcode running time is about 8 minutes.

Please tighten the 3 screws that secure the heatbed.

![](/n1/bed-leveling/3_screws.jpeg)

It’s recommended to insert the key first, then hold the key and the build plate with your hand to prevent the screws from falling out.

![](/a1m/manual/unbox/%E7%94%A8%E6%89%8B%E5%9B%BA%E5%AE%9A.jpg)

**The A1 mini Bed Tramming is completed.**

## [¶](#calibration) Calibration

From the printer screen, touch **Settings > Maintenance > Calibration** to start the calibration. If everything looks good and no errors or warnings appear during the calibration, the leveling was successful. If there are still problems, contact the service team for further assistance.

![](/a1/maintenance/bed-tramming/20250116-093750.jpg)

## [¶](#end-notes) End Notes

> We hope the detailed guide provided has been helpful and informative.
>
> To ensure a safe and effective execution, if you have any concerns or questions about the process described in this article, we recommend submitting a [technical ticket](https://bambulab.com/en/my/support/tickets?from=5) regarding your issue.
>
> Please include a picture or video illustrating the problem, as well as any additional information related to your inquiry.

---

A1 mini Belt Tensioning

This guide shows how to re-tension the X/Y/Z belts of the A1 mini printer.

---

There are 2 timing belts on the A1 mini printer, which are transmission components that enable the toolhead (X), the heatbed (Y), and the X-axis (Z) to move in the set direction. Timely tightening of the belts is an essential guarantee for the print quality.

During the calibration process, the A1 mini performs a frequency scan to determine whether the belts need to be retensioned. If retensioning is necessary, a relevant prompt will appear; please follow the instructions to adjust the corresponding belts.

The printer's X/Y/Z axes determine the zero point coordinates of the toolhead in three-dimensional space by detecting the limits of each axis. If the position during the homing process deviates significantly or if there are frequent step loss, it is advisable to adjust the tension of the timing belts.

![](https://wiki.bambulab.com/n1/maintenance/belt_tension/a1_mini_%E5%90%8C%E6%AD%A5%E5%B8%A6%E5%BC%A0%E7%B4%A7.jpg)

## [¶](#tools-required) Tools required

* H2.0 Allen Key
* 15 minutes

## [¶](#safety-warning) Safety warning

> **Important!**
>
> It's crucial to **power off the printer before conducting any maintenance work**, including work on the printer's electronics and tool head wires. Performing tasks with the printer on can result in a short circuit, leading to electronic damage and safety hazards.
>
> During maintenance or troubleshooting, you may need to disassemble parts, including the hotend. This exposes wires and electrical components that could short circuit if they contact each other, other metal, or electronic components while the printer is still on. **This can result in damage to the printer's electronics and additional issues**.
>
> Therefore, it's crucial to **turn off the printer and disconnect it from the power source before conducting any maintenance**. This prevents short circuits or damage to the printer's electronics, ensuring safe and effective maintenance.
>
> For any concerns or questions about following this guide, please [open a new ticket in our Support Page](https://bambulab.com/en/my/support/tickets?from=5) and we will do our best to respond promptly and provide the assistance you need.

## [¶](#x-belt-tensioning) X Belt Tensioning

### [¶](#location-of-the-tensioning-screw) Location of the tensioning screw

The tensioning position of the X belt is on the back of the toolhead, as shown in the following figure.

![](https://wiki.bambulab.com/a1/maintenance/belt_tension/x_screw.jpg)

### [¶](#operation-guide) Operation guide

1. Operate the printer to raise the X-axis to the middle position of the Z-axis to avoid scratching the heatbed when moving the toolhead;

![安装完成.png](https://wiki.bambulab.com/n1/a1-mini-print-head/%E6%8B%86%E8%A3%85/%E5%AE%89%E8%A3%85%E5%AE%8C%E6%88%90.png)

2. Loosen the tension screw with an H2.0 Allen key by one turn (do not fully loosen them), and move the toolhead back and forth along the X-axis 3~4 times by hand, then retighten the tensioning screw.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](/a1/maintenance/belt_tension/x_screw.jpg) | ![](/n1/maintenance/belt_tension/20250120-120627.jpg) |

## [¶](#y-belt-tensioning) Y Belt Tensioning

### [¶](#location-of-the-tensioning-screws) Location of the tensioning screws

The tensioning screws for the Y axis belt are located in front of the bottom base, covered by a small cap, which needs to be removed first.

![](/n1/maintenance/belt_tension/y%E5%90%8C%E6%AD%A5%E5%B8%A6%E5%BC%A0%E7%B4%A7.jpg)

### [¶](#operation-guide-1) Operation guide

1. Remove 2 screws and take off the Y-axis rear cover.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](/n1/maintenance/belt_tension/y%E5%90%8C%E6%AD%A5%E5%B8%A6%E5%BC%A0%E7%B4%A7.jpg) | ![](/n1/maintenance/belt_tension/%E6%89%93%E5%BC%80%E7%AB%AF%E7%9B%96.jpg) |

2. Loosen the tension screw (the screw inside the green circle below); gently push the Y-Axis Tensioner to ensure it is not stuck; after releasing the Y-Axis Tensioner, re-tighten the tension screw.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](https://wiki.bambulab.com/n1/maintenance/belt_tension/y%E5%BC%A0%E7%B4%A7%E8%9E%BA%E4%B8%9D.jpg) | ![](/n1/maintenance/belt_tension/%E5%BC%A0%E7%B4%A7%E5%9D%97.jpg) |

## [¶](#calibration) Calibration

After both the X and Y belts are tensioned, open the printer, select **Vibration Compensation** in the **Calibration** menu to complete a calibration process.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](/n1/maintenance/belt_tension/20250120-145929.jpg) | ![](/n1/maintenance/belt_tension/screen2.jpg) |

In addition, all series of Bambu printers can be calibrated through Bambu Studio.

![](/n1/maintenance/belt_tension/studio.jpg)

## [¶](#end-notes) **End Notes**

> We hope the detailed guide provided has been helpful and informative.
>
> If this guide does not solve your problem, please submit a [technical ticket](https://bambulab.com/en/my/support/tickets/create?from=5), we will answer your questions and provide assistance.
>
> If you have any suggestions or feedback on this Wiki, please leave a message in the comment area. Thank you for your support and attention!

# A1 series extruder cleaning tutorial | Bambu Lab Wiki

---

A1 series extruder cleaning tutorial

This article introduces how to clean the A1 extruder

---

## [¶](#when-to-use) When to use

After using the printer for a long time, filament debris and dust may remain inside the extruder, which can easily cause the extrusion gear to become stuck or even unable to rotate during operation. In order to ensure the normal printing of the printer, the extruder needs to be cleaned of foreign materials on time.

## [¶](#tools-and-materials-needed) Tools and materials needed

* Tweezers
* Brush
* H1.5 and 2.0 Allen key
* Air blower

## [¶](#general-cleaning) General cleaning

### [¶](#step-1-remove-the-hotend-assembly) Step 1-Remove the hotend assembly

(If the filament is in loading status, please use the filament cutter to cut off it first) Press the Toolhead Cover from both sides to unlock and remove the cover. Pull the protrusion on the side of the Silicone Sock for Hotend with your hands, and remove the Silicone Sock for Hotend .

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](https://wiki.bambulab.com/n1/a1-mini-print-head/remove_cover.jpg) | ![](/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/%E6%AD%A5%E9%AA%A41-%E7%A7%BB%E9%99%A4%E5%96%B7%E5%98%B4%E7%BB%84%E4%BB%B6-2.png) |

Remove the rotating wheel. Take off the pressure block by using a H1.5 Allen key wrench to unscrew two screws.

|  |  |  |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ![](/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/%E6%AD%A5%E9%AA%A41-%E7%A7%BB%E9%99%A4%E5%96%B7%E5%98%B4%E7%BB%84%E4%BB%B6-3.jpeg) | ![](/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/%E6%AD%A5%E9%AA%A41-%E7%A7%BB%E9%99%A4%E5%96%B7%E5%98%B4%E7%BB%84%E4%BB%B6-4.jpeg) | ![](/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/%E6%AD%A5%E9%AA%A41-%E7%A7%BB%E9%99%A4%E5%96%B7%E5%98%B4%E7%BB%84%E4%BB%B6-5.jpeg) |

Release the hotend latch, then hold the heat sink with your hands and take out the hotend.

|  |  |  |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ![](/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/%E6%AD%A5%E9%AA%A41-%E7%A7%BB%E9%99%A4%E5%96%B7%E5%98%B4%E7%BB%84%E4%BB%B6-6.jpeg) | ![](/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/%E6%AD%A5%E9%AA%A41-%E7%A7%BB%E9%99%A4%E5%96%B7%E5%98%B4%E7%BB%84%E4%BB%B6-7.jpeg) | ![](/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/%E6%AD%A5%E9%AA%A41-%E7%A7%BB%E9%99%A4%E5%96%B7%E5%98%B4%E7%BB%84%E4%BB%B6-8.jpeg) |

### [¶](#step-2-loosen-the-filament-cutter) Step 2-Loosen the filament cutter

Use a H2.0 Allen key wrench to unscrew one screw, release the filament cutter.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/%E6%AD%A5%E9%AA%A42-%E6%8B%A7%E6%9D%BE%E5%88%87%E5%88%80-1.png) | ![](/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/%E6%AD%A5%E9%AA%A42-%E6%8B%A7%E6%9D%BE%E5%88%87%E5%88%80-2.png) |

Use tweezers and a blower to clean the internal filament debris and residue from the indicated positions (manually rotate the yellow gear clockwise for cleaning while the power is off, or click the "retract" button on the screen while the power is on to rotate the extruder wheel to the specified angle for cleaning)

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/%E6%AD%A5%E9%AA%A42-%E6%8B%A7%E6%9D%BE%E5%88%87%E5%88%80-3.png) | ![](/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/%E6%AD%A5%E9%AA%A42-%E6%8B%A7%E6%9D%BE%E5%88%87%E5%88%80-4.png) |

When cleaning the extruder according to the above steps fails to achieve satisfactory results, you can disassemble the extruder and remove the extrusion wheel assembly for cleaning.

## [¶](#deep-cleaning) Deep cleaning

### [¶](#step-3-remove-the-filament-hub-assembly) Step 3-Remove the filament hub assembly

Use an H2.0 Allen key wrench to unscrew two screws and then take out the filament hub. Please be careful not to damage the black FPC cable. After removing the filament hub, you can place it on top of the tool head as shown in the picture.

|  |  |  |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ![](/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/%E6%AD%A5%E9%AA%A43-%E7%A7%BB%E9%99%A4%E4%BA%94%E9%80%9A-1.png) | ![](/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/%E6%AD%A5%E9%AA%A43-%E7%A7%BB%E9%99%A4%E4%BA%94%E9%80%9A-2.png) | ![](/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/%E6%AD%A5%E9%AA%A43-%E7%A7%BB%E9%99%A4%E4%BA%94%E9%80%9A-3.png) |

### [¶](#step-4-remove-the-extruder-front-cover) Step 4-Remove the extruder front cover

Use a H2.0 Allen key wrench to unscrew the 4 screws on the front cover of the extruder.

|  |  |  |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ![](/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/%E6%AD%A5%E9%AA%A44-%E7%A7%BB%E9%99%A4%E6%8C%A4%E5%87%BA%E6%9C%BA%E5%89%8D%E7%9B%96-1.png) | ![](/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/%E6%AD%A5%E9%AA%A44-%E7%A7%BB%E9%99%A4%E6%8C%A4%E5%87%BA%E6%9C%BA%E5%89%8D%E7%9B%96-2.png) | ![](/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/%E6%AD%A5%E9%AA%A44-%E7%A7%BB%E9%99%A4%E6%8C%A4%E5%87%BA%E6%9C%BA%E5%89%8D%E7%9B%96-3.png) |

When removing the front cover, first loosen the screw on the side (only loosen it slightly). Then, hold the top of the bracket and remove the front cover of the extruder while holding the other side.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/%E6%AD%A5%E9%AA%A44-%E7%A7%BB%E9%99%A4%E6%8C%A4%E5%87%BA%E6%9C%BA%E5%89%8D%E7%9B%96-4.png) | ![](/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/%E6%AD%A5%E9%AA%A44-%E7%A7%BB%E9%99%A4%E6%8C%A4%E5%87%BA%E6%9C%BA%E5%89%8D%E7%9B%96-5.png) |

### [¶](#step-5-remove-the-driven-wheel-bracket) Step 5-Remove the driven wheel bracket

Use a H2.0 hex key wrench to unscrew the screw on the side and carefully take out the bracket. **Please be cautious when handling it as the internal spring and end cap can easily fall off. Use tweezers to remove them carefully to prevent loss.**

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/%E6%AD%A5%E9%AA%A45-%E7%A7%BB%E9%99%A4%E4%BB%8E%E5%8A%A8%E8%BD%AE%E6%94%AF%E6%9E%B6-1.png) | ![](/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/%E6%AD%A5%E9%AA%A45-%E7%A7%BB%E9%99%A4%E4%BB%8E%E5%8A%A8%E8%BD%AE%E6%94%AF%E6%9E%B6-2.png) |

**Pay close attention not to lose this metallic cap, as it is important**

![](/metallic_cap.jpeg)

### [¶](#step-6-remove-the-extrusion-gear) Step 6-Remove the extrusion gear

Remove the extruder gear assembly directly and clean the residue of filaments inside the extruder.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/%E6%AD%A5%E9%AA%A46-%E7%A7%BB%E9%99%A4%E6%8C%A4%E5%87%BA%E9%BD%BF%E8%BD%AE-1.png) | ![](/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/%E6%AD%A5%E9%AA%A46-%E7%A7%BB%E9%99%A4%E6%8C%A4%E5%87%BA%E9%BD%BF%E8%BD%AE-2.jpg) |

## [¶](#assembly-instructions) Assembly instructions

### [¶](#step-1-install-the-extrusion-gear) Step 1-Install the extrusion gear

Install the extrusion gear.

![](/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/%E7%BB%84%E8%A3%85%E6%AD%A5%E9%AA%A41-%E5%AE%89%E8%A3%85%E6%8C%A4%E5%87%BA%E9%BD%BF%E8%BD%AE.jpg)

### [¶](#step-2-install-the-driven-wheel-bracket) Step 2-Install the driven wheel bracket

Install the driven wheel bracket to the corresponding shaft. Assemble the spring and end cap together **in the correct position and direction** (see the images below for the correct and wrong examples), and carefully place them in the corresponding position of the driven wheel bracket using tweezers. **Do not tighten the screw on the side for the moment.**

|  |  |  |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ![](https://wiki.bambulab.com/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/%E7%BB%84%E8%A3%85%E6%AD%A5%E9%AA%A42-%E5%AE%89%E8%A3%85%E4%BB%8E%E5%8A%A8%E8%BD%AE%E6%94%AF%E6%9E%B6-1.jpg) | ![](https://wiki.bambulab.com/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/%E7%BB%84%E8%A3%85%E6%AD%A5%E9%AA%A42-%E5%AE%89%E8%A3%85%E4%BB%8E%E5%8A%A8%E8%BD%AE%E6%94%AF%E6%9E%B6-2.jpg) | ![](https://wiki.bambulab.com/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/%E7%BB%84%E8%A3%85%E6%AD%A5%E9%AA%A42-%E5%AE%89%E8%A3%85%E4%BB%8E%E5%8A%A8%E8%BD%AE%E6%94%AF%E6%9E%B6-3.jpg) |

> **Important!**
>
> **Please make sure to install the spring and end cap correctly. Otherwise, the gear will not be able to grip on the filament, causing print failure.**

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/correct-.png)   **Correct installation** | ![](/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/wrong-.png)   **Wrong installation** |

### [¶](#step-3-install-the-extruder-unit-front-cover) Step 3-Install the extruder unit front cover

Tighten the 4 screws on the front cover of the extruder.

![](/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/%E7%BB%84%E8%A3%85%E6%AD%A5%E9%AA%A43-%E5%AE%89%E8%A3%85%E6%8C%A4%E5%87%BA%E6%9C%BA%E5%89%8D%E7%9B%96.jpg)

### [¶](#step-4-tighten-the-screw-on-the-side) Step 4-Tighten the screw on the side

Tighten the screw on the side of the extruder.

![](/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/tighten_screw_on_the_side_en.jpg)

### [¶](#step-5-install-the-filament-hub-assembly) Step 5-Install the filament hub assembly

Install the filament hub assembly, make sure that the black FPC cable is not folded and smoothly placed in the vacant position. Then, tighten the 2 screws to secure thefilament hub in place.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/%E7%BB%84%E8%A3%85%E6%AD%A5%E9%AA%A44-%E5%AE%89%E8%A3%85%E4%BA%94%E9%80%9A-1.png) | ![](/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/%E7%BB%84%E8%A3%85%E6%AD%A5%E9%AA%A44-%E5%AE%89%E8%A3%85%E4%BA%94%E9%80%9A-2.png) |

### [¶](#step-6-install-the-filament-cutter) Step 6-Install the filament cutter

Lift the lever and slide the cutter into place along the blade groove. Secure the cutter lever by tightening 1 screw.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/%E7%BB%84%E8%A3%85%E6%AD%A5%E9%AA%A45-%E5%AE%89%E8%A3%85%E5%88%87%E5%88%80-1.png) | ![](/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/%E7%BB%84%E8%A3%85%E6%AD%A5%E9%AA%A45-%E5%AE%89%E8%A3%85%E5%88%87%E5%88%80-2.png) |

### [¶](#step-7-install-the-hotend-assembly) Step 7-Install the hotend assembly

Hold the nozzle heat sink, align the mounting position, mount the nozzle in place, and snap the quick-release device. Put on the Silicone Sock for Hotend and tighten the 2 screws to secure the pressure block in place.

|  |  |  |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ![](/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/%E7%BB%84%E8%A3%85%E6%AD%A5%E9%AA%A46-%E5%AE%89%E8%A3%85%E5%96%B7%E5%98%B4%E7%BB%84%E4%BB%B6-1.jpg) | ![](/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/%E7%BB%84%E8%A3%85%E6%AD%A5%E9%AA%A46-%E5%AE%89%E8%A3%85%E5%96%B7%E5%98%B4%E7%BB%84%E4%BB%B6-2.jpg) | ![](/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/%E7%BB%84%E8%A3%85%E6%AD%A5%E9%AA%A46-%E5%AE%89%E8%A3%85%E5%96%B7%E5%98%B4%E7%BB%84%E4%BB%B6-3.jpg) |

Install the rotating wheel and toolhead front cover.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/%E7%BB%84%E8%A3%85%E6%AD%A5%E9%AA%A46-%E5%AE%89%E8%A3%85%E5%96%B7%E5%98%B4%E7%BB%84%E4%BB%B6-4.jpg) | ![](/n1/troubleshooting/extruder-clog/%E7%BB%84%E8%A3%85%E6%AD%A5%E9%AA%A46-%E5%AE%89%E8%A3%85%E5%96%B7%E5%98%B4%E7%BB%84%E4%BB%B6-5.jpg) |

---

Nozzle stuck – hotend residue adhesion

the hotend may become stuck to the heating block due to filament adhesion; this article explains how to safely remove the nozzle.

---

## [¶](#hotend-and-heating-assembly) Hotend and Heating Assembly

A small amount of filament may remain between the quick-release hotend and the heating assembly, making it impossible to remove the nozzle at low temperatures. This article uses the A1 as an example, and the operating steps for all quick-release hotends are the same.

## [¶](#required-tools-and-materials) Required Tools and Materials

* Heat-insulating gloves
* Tweezers
* Paper towels

## [¶](#operation-steps) Operation Steps

### [¶](#safety-warning) Safety Warning

> Before performing any maintenance work on the printer and its electronic devices (including the toolhead cable), turn off the printer power and disconnect the power supply to avoid accidental screen touches or circuit short circuits that may cause additional electronic device damage and safety hazards.
> When performing maintenance or troubleshooting on the printer, first confirm the temperature of the hotend and heatbed. Avoid operating at high temperatures. If you must operate at high temperatures, wear heat-insulating gloves to ensure safe and effective maintenance work.

### [¶](#remove-the-hotend) Remove the Hotend

Press the filament cutter lever to cut off any possible filament.

![按压切刀1.jpg](/knowledge-sharing/troubleshooting-hotend-removal-lssues/%E6%8C%89%E5%8E%8B%E5%88%87%E5%88%801.jpg)

Set the nozzle temperature to 220℃ via the screen.

![217度en.jpg](/knowledge-sharing/troubleshooting-hotend-removal-lssues/217%E5%BA%A6en.jpg)

After the temperature rises, use tweezers to pry open the heating assembly locking tab.

![打开卡扣.jpg](/knowledge-sharing/troubleshooting-hotend-removal-lssues/%E6%89%93%E5%BC%80%E5%8D%A1%E6%89%A3.jpg)

Then clamp and take out the nozzle.

![取下喷嘴.jpg](/knowledge-sharing/troubleshooting-hotend-removal-lssues/%E5%8F%96%E4%B8%8B%E5%96%B7%E5%98%B4.jpg)

### [¶](#clean-the-hotend) Clean the Hotend

> Wear heat-insulating gloves to complete this cleaning work, and pay attention to high-temperature scalds.

Wipe the nozzle surface firmly with a paper towel to ensure the nozzle is clean.

![清理喷嘴1.webp](/knowledge-sharing/troubleshooting-hotend-removal-lssues/%E6%B8%85%E7%90%86%E5%96%B7%E5%98%B41.webp)

Then wipe the base of the heating assembly to ensure there is no obvious debris.

![清理加热组件2.jpg](/knowledge-sharing/troubleshooting-hotend-removal-lssues/%E6%B8%85%E7%90%86%E5%8A%A0%E7%83%AD%E7%BB%84%E4%BB%B62.jpg)

### [¶](#install-the-hotend-and-silicone-sleeve) Install the Hotend and Silicone Sleeve

**Wait for the hotend and heating assembly to cool down after cleaning**, and place the hotend into the heating assembly after cooling.

![常温en.jpg](/knowledge-sharing/troubleshooting-hotend-removal-lssues/%E5%B8%B8%E6%B8%A9en.jpg)
 ![安装喷嘴1.jpg](/knowledge-sharing/troubleshooting-hotend-removal-lssues/%E5%AE%89%E8%A3%85%E5%96%B7%E5%98%B41.jpg)

Fasten the heating assembly locking tab correctly.

![安装卡扣1.jpg](/knowledge-sharing/troubleshooting-hotend-removal-lssues/%E5%AE%89%E8%A3%85%E5%8D%A1%E6%89%A31.jpg)

Install the silicone sleeve.

![安装硅胶套1.jpg](/knowledge-sharing/troubleshooting-hotend-removal-lssues/%E5%AE%89%E8%A3%85%E7%A1%85%E8%83%B6%E5%A5%971.jpg)

## [¶](#end-notes) End Notes

> We hope this guide has provided clear and practical support.
> If the issue remains unresolved, please submit a [support ticket](https://bambulab.com/en/my/support/tickets/create) and include your recent printer logs and additional pictures or other details. Our technical team will review your request and provide detailed assistance.
> You can also visit [Bambu AI](https://support.bambulab.com/en?from=6&lang=en-us), which can instantly answer common questions and provide you with operational guidance.

---

Hotend Clumping/Blob Cleaning Instructions-A1

A page to guide you how to clear the hotend cluming/blob of filament for A1

---

## [¶](#hotend-clumping) Hotend Clumping

Hotend Clumping, also called Hotend Blob is a mass of plastic accumulated around the hotend. When the model detaches during the first few layers of printing for various reasons, it may adhere to the nozzle. As the nozzle continues to extrude, this can result in a more serious “clumping” issue.

![](/n1/maintenance/hotend_blob/%E8%A3%B9%E5%A4%B4.webp)

This can be one of the most challenging issues you may face when 3D printing. Removing it can be tricky, but there are methods you can try to clean it up.

In order to help and guide users to complete the cleaning operation, we have prepared this wiki, hoping to help you confirm the damage and reduce secondary damage to the parts on it.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](/n1/maintenance/hotend_blob/blob1.jpg) | ![](/n1/maintenance/hotend_blob/blob3.jpg) |

This problem usually occurs when the plate is not correctly cleaned before printing. [**We recommend following this Wiki article**](/en/filament-acc/acc/pei-plate-clean-guide) **for guidance on how to clean the plate before starting a print to avoid this issue in the future.**

## [¶](#video-guide) Video Guide

## [¶](#preparation) Preparation

### [¶](#h-1-tools) 1. Tools

* Hair dryer (High-temperature setting)
* Flat head tweezers
* Paper
* Heat resistant gloves
* H1.5/H2.0 Allen key

![](/n1/maintenance/hotend_blob/tools.jpg)

### [¶](#h-2-set-the-printer-status) 2. Set the printer status

#### [¶](#raise-the-x-axis-and-move-the-heatbed-forward) Raise the X-axis and move the heatbed forward

To create more working space, raise the X-axis higher using the on-screen buttons.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](https://wiki.bambulab.com/blob-cleaning/%E5%8D%87%E9%AB%98-control.png) | ![](https://wiki.bambulab.com/blob-cleaning/xyz.png) |
| ![](https://wiki.bambulab.com/blob-cleaning/%E6%8F%90%E9%AB%98x%E8%BD%B4.png) | ![](/blob-cleaning/cancel.png) |

#### [¶](#enter-printer-maintenance-mode) Enter printer maintenance mode

To perform the cleaning operation, enable the Maintenance Mode. This will allow the hotend to remain heated during the operation.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](https://wiki.bambulab.com/blob-cleaning/setting.png) | ![](https://wiki.bambulab.com/blob-cleaning/maintenance.png) |
| ![](https://wiki.bambulab.com/blob-cleaning/%E7%BB%B4%E6%8A%A4%E6%A8%A1%E5%BC%8F.png) | ![](https://wiki.bambulab.com/blob-cleaning/%E6%89%93%E5%BC%80%E7%BB%B4%E6%8A%A4%E6%A8%A1%E5%BC%8F.png) |

#### [¶](#heating-the-hotend) Heating the hotend

Set the hotend temperature slightly higher than the printing temperature of the material used before, to soften the blob. Taking PLA as an example, the hotend can be heated to around 250 degrees Celsius.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](/blob-cleaning/control.png) | ![](/blob-cleaning/nozzle.png) |
| ![](/blob-cleaning/%E5%8A%A0%E7%83%AD.png) | ![](/blob-cleaning/%E5%8A%A0%E7%83%AD%E5%AE%8C%E6%88%90.png) |

> **Note: For safety reasons, when the printer is not in its normal working condition, the hotend will automatically stop heating after being maintained at a high temperature for a certain period of time. If you have not completed the necessary processing within this time, please remember to reheat the hotend.**

## [¶](#safety-warning) Safety Warning

> **IMPORTANT!**
>
> When you perform maintenance or troubleshooting on the printer, please first confirm the temperature of the hot end and hot bed to avoid operating at high temperatures. If you must operate at high temperatures, please wear heat-insulating gloves to ensure safe and effective execution.
>
> If you have any concerns or questions about following this guide, [open a new ticket in our Support Page](https://bambulab.com/en/my/support/tickets?from=5) and we will do our best to respond promptly and provide you with the assistance you need.

## [¶](#clear-the-clumpingblob) **Clear the Clumping/Blob**

After completing the aforementioned preparations, you can proceed to clear the clumping from the hotend.

> During the cleaning process, avoid removing the toolhead from the X-axis linear rail as much as possible, as this may cause the steel balls inside the slider to fall out.

### [¶](#remove-the-fans) Remove the Fans

Step 1. Remove the front cover of the toolhead.

![](/blob-cleaning/%E5%B7%A5%E5%85%B7%E5%A4%B4%E5%89%8D%E7%9B%96.png)

Step 2. Remove the two screws holding the hotend fan.

![](/blob-cleaning/%E6%8B%86%E9%99%A4%E4%B8%A4%E9%A2%97%E8%9E%BA%E4%B8%9D.png)

Step 3. Remove the three screws holding the part cooling fan.

![](/blob-cleaning/%E6%8B%86%E9%99%A4%E4%B8%89%E9%A2%97%E8%9E%BA%E4%B8%9D.png)

Step 4. Gently pull the part cooling fan downwards. The heated hotend will make it easier to remove a minor clump.

![](/blob-cleaning/%E6%8B%86%E9%99%A4%E9%83%A8%E4%BB%B6%E5%86%B7%E5%8D%B4%E9%A3%8E%E6%89%87.png)

If the hotend clumping is severe and cannot be directly removed, you can forcibly pull off the part cooling fan (which may damage the screw holes), then unscrew the four screws from the back to remove the hotend heating assembly.

![](/n1/maintenance/hotend_blob/%E8%A3%B9%E5%A4%B4%E5%BC%BA%E8%A1%8C%E6%8B%86%E9%83%A8%E4%BB%B6%E5%86%B7%E5%8D%B4%E9%A3%8E%E6%89%87.jpg)

### [¶](#clean-the-silicone-hotend-sock) Clean the Silicone Hotend Sock

Step 1. Using the heat resistant gloves, remove the silicone sock and clean it.

![](/blob-cleaning/%E6%88%B4%E4%B8%8A%E7%A1%85%E8%83%B6%E6%89%8B%E5%A5%97.png)

Step 2. This is the silicone sock after cleaning. Ensure no filament is left on it.

![](/blob-cleaning/%E4%BF%9D%E8%AF%81%E6%B2%A1%E6%9C%89%E4%BB%BB%E4%BD%95%E6%AE%8B%E7%95%99.png)

### [¶](#clean-the-hotend) Clean the Hotend

Step 1. Press the filament cutter lever, to cut the filament.

![](/blob-cleaning/%E6%8C%89%E4%B8%8B%E5%88%87%E5%88%80.png)

Step 2. While the hotend is heated, open the hotend buckle and remove the nozzle.

![](/blob-cleaning/%E6%89%93%E5%BC%80%E5%8D%A1%E6%89%A3.png)

Step 3. Right after removing the nozzle, start cleaning the filament while it's soft, until the nozzle is free from any debris.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](https://wiki.bambulab.com/blob-cleaning/%E6%B8%85%E7%90%86.png) | ![](https://wiki.bambulab.com/blob-cleaning/%E4%BF%9D%E8%AF%81%E6%B2%A1%E6%9C%89%E4%BB%BB%E4%BD%95%E6%AE%8B%E7%95%99.png) |

### [¶](#clean-the-hotend-heating-assembly) Clean the Hotend Heating Assembly

Step 1. Carefully remove all the soft filament around the hotend heating assembly until there is no residue left.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](https://wiki.bambulab.com/blob-cleaning/%E6%B8%85%E6%A5%9A%E5%91%A8%E5%9B%B4%E6%89%80%E6%9C%89%E8%80%97%E6%9D%90.png) | ![](https://wiki.bambulab.com/blob-cleaning/%E6%B8%85%E7%90%86%E5%AE%8C%E6%88%90.png) |

Step 2. Set the hotend temperature to 0℃ and wait for it to cool.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](/blob-cleaning/%E7%83%AD%E7%AB%AF-control.png) | ![](/blob-cleaning/%E7%83%AD%E7%AB%AF%E8%AE%BE%E7%BD%AE.png) |
| ![](/blob-cleaning/%E8%AE%BE%E7%BD%AE%E4%B8%BA0%E5%BA%A6.png) | ![](/blob-cleaning/nozzle_-34.png) |

### [¶](#clean-the-parts-cooling-fan) Clean the Parts Cooling Fan

Step 1. Use the hair dryer at higher heating temperatures and maintain the heat for 1-2 minutes until the blob starts to become soft.

![](/blob-cleaning/%E5%90%B9%E9%A3%8E%E6%9C%BA.png)

> **Be careful when using the heairdryer or hot gun on the plastic parts for too long or too near**. According to actual policy, the warranty does not support the replacement of this part.

Step 2. When the blob is soft, gently pull it away from the part cooling fan.

![](/blob-cleaning/%E5%B0%86%E5%85%B6%E6%8B%89%E7%A6%BB%E9%A3%8E%E6%89%87.png)

> **Additionally, cease heating the hotend at this point to prevent the risk of burns during the operation.**

### [¶](#assembly) Assembly

Step 1. Once the treatment is completed and there is no apparent damage, reinstall the previously removed parts.

![](/blob-cleaning/%E9%87%8D%E6%96%B0%E5%AE%89%E8%A3%85%E6%8B%86%E9%99%A4%E7%9A%84%E9%83%A8%E5%88%86.png)

Step 2. Re-install the nozzle and secure it with the buckle, then attach the silicone sock

![](/blob-cleaning/%E5%AE%89%E8%A3%85%E5%A5%BD%E5%89%8D%E7%9B%96-%E7%A1%85%E8%83%B6%E5%A5%97.png)

Step 3. Reinstall the toolhead front cover.

![](/blob-cleaning/%E5%AE%89%E8%A3%85%E5%B7%A5%E5%85%B7%E5%A4%B4%E5%89%8D%E7%9B%96.png)

Step 4. After completing the assembly, refer to the maintenance mode setup instructions from the preparation stage to exit maintenance mode.

![](/blob-cleaning/20240913-180222.jpg)

> **Note: If any damage is detected, the necessary parts can be purchased through the online store.**

## [¶](#end-notes) End notes

> We hope that the detailed guide we shared with you was helpful and informative.
>
> We want to ensure that you can perform it safely and effectively. If you have any concerns or questions regarding the process described in this article, we encourage you to reach out to our friendly customer service team before starting the operation. Our team is always ready to help you and answer any questions you may have.
>
> [Click here to open a new ticket in our Support Page](https://bambulab.com/en/my/support/tickets?from=5).
>
> We will do our best to respond promptly and provide you with the assistance you need.

A1 mini Maintenance Guidelines

A collection of recommendations for various maintenance recommended to be done on Bambu Lab A1 mini.

---

## [¶](#a1-mini-maintenance-guidelines) A1 mini Maintenance Guidelines

Regular maintenance can minimize wear and extend the printer's lifespan while also lowering the risk of a print failure. To help ease your purchasing decision, we have listed all the commonly needed maintenance parts and offered tiered discounts. You can find the purchase link on [Spare Parts Page](https://store.bambulab.com/collections/spare-parts-for-a1-series).

## [¶](#safety-warning) Safety Warning

> **⚠️ IMPORTANT!**
>
> It's crucial to **power off the printer before conducting any maintenance work**, including work on the printer's electronics and tool head wires. Performing tasks with the printer on can result in a short circuit, leading to electronic damage and safety hazards.
>
> During maintenance or troubleshooting, you may need to disassemble parts, including the hotend. This exposes wires and electrical components that could short circuit if they contact each other, other metal, or electronic components while the printer is still on. **This can result in damage to the printer's electronics and additional issues.**
>
> Therefore, it's crucial to **turn off the printer and disconnect it from the power source before conducting any maintenance**. This prevents short circuits or damage to the printer's electronics, ensuring safe and effective maintenance.

## [¶](#moving-parts) Moving parts

Refers to the components that undergo mechanical movement during the printing process, including screw rods, guide rails, sliders, couplings, belts, fans, etc. The main maintenance measures are cleaning and lubrication. Metal parts need to be rust-proofed. Rubber parts need to be checked for aging (including deformation), wear, etc., and replaced when necessary.

### [¶](#guide-rail-system) Guide rail system

![](/n1/p-maintenance/axises.png)

We spent extra to bring you the most reliable All-metal rails and cover-protected drive system. This rail system ensures that you do not need to frequently adjust the moving components of the A1 mini, but requires regular lubrication and cleaning to reduce wear and noise, ensure the accuracy and lifespan of the printer, and effectively prevent the guide rails from rusting.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| X rail | When: Every month    Materials & tools: Cloth,Tweezers,Lubricant oil    Solution: Cleaning, Lubricate    How to do: Use tweezers to clean the lumpy and filamentous foreign objects on the guide rail, and wipe the granular foreign objects and stains with a dust-free cloth; re-lubricate the X track, and slide the tool head back and forth after lubricating to ensure that the lubricant lubricates the entire linear rail. |
| Z rail | When: Every month    Materials & tools: Cloth,Tweezers,Lubricant oil    Solution: Cleaning, Lubricate    How to do: Use tweezers to clean the lumpy and filamentous foreign matter on the guide rail, and wipe the granular foreign matter and stains with a dust-free cloth; re-lubricate the Z rail, and the oil filling hole in the middle of the slider can also be lubricated. Pay attention to cleaning up foreign matter in the groove of the linear rail. After applying oil, turn on the printer and control the Z-axis to move up and down to ensure that the lubricant oil lubricates the entire linear rail. |
| Y rail | When: Every month    Materials & tools: Cloth,Tweezers,Lubricant oil    Solution: Cleaning, Lubricate    How to do: Use tweezers to clean the lumpy and filamentous foreign objects on the guide rail, and wipe the granular foreign objects and stains with a dust-free cloth; re-lubricate the Y rails on both sides, and slide the heatbed back and forth after lubricating to ensure that the lubricant lubricates the entire Y rail. |

<https://public-cdn.bblmw.com/wiki/A1-mini/x_guide_rail_lubrication.mp4>

### [¶](#x-y-timing-belts) X Y timing belts

![](/n1/p-maintenance/belts.png)

A1 mini can check the belt tension automatically through the vibration frequency calibration. If the belt is loose, the HMS system will notify you to use the auto belt tensioner to set the belt to the right tension.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| X Belt | When: HMS prompts    Materials & tools: H2 Allen key    Solution: Re-tension    How to do: Loosen the tensioning screw behind the tool head, move the toolhead along the X rail, and re-tighten the tightening screw. |
| Y Belt | When: HMS prompts    Materials & tools: H2 Allen key    Solution: Re-tension    How to do: After removing the Y-axis rear cover, loosen the tensioning screw, push the Y-axis tensioning slider forcefully along the Y-axis 2 to 3 times to ensure that it is not stuck, and then re-tighten the tightening screw. |

![](/n1/p-maintenance/x_belt_t_1.2.png)

Loosen the tensioning screw

|  |  |  |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ![](/n1/p-maintenance/y_belt_t_1.png)  Remove the Y-axis rear cover | ![](/n1/p-maintenance/y_belt_t_2.png)  Loosen the tensioning screw (the one in the green circle below) | ![](/n1/p-maintenance/press_the_tensioner_block.jpg)  Push the Y tensioning block and re-lock the tensioning screw |

If after long-term use, the timing belt has shown obvious wear and affects the print quality, please refer to the relevant maintenance guide page to replace the timing belt.

[X-axis installation installation tutorial](/en/a1-mini/maintenance/x-axis)

[A1 mini Y-axis & Z-axis Assembly Installation Tutorial](/en/a1-mini/maintenance/yz-axis)

### [¶](#lead-screw) Lead screw

There is only one lead screw in the A1 mini. Through the lead screw, the Z-axis motor can drive the X-axis to move up and down along the Z-axis direction. Proper lubrication will ensure the smooth operation of the X-axis along the Z direction, and can effectively reduce the noise caused by the movement of the screw.

**Unlike linear rails, lead screws do not necessarily require lubricant oil.** You can use either lubricant oil or lubricant grease. If you have access to lubricant grease, we recommend using it because lubricant grease is less likely to evaporate.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| Lead Screw | When: Every 3 months    Materials & tools: Lubricant grease or oil     Solution: Lubricate    How to do: Apply grease to the working area and the bottom bearing, turn on the printer, and control the printer to move the X-axis up and down along the Z direction to ensure that the entire lead screw working area is coated with grease. |

Video guide for lubricating the lead screws:

<https://public-cdn.bblmw.com/wiki/A1-mini/lead_screw_maintenance.mp4>

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](/n1/p-maintenance/oil_en.jpg) | ![](/n1/p-maintenance/z_screw.png) |

If necessary, you can tilt the printer slightly and then apply lubricant oil to the lead screw.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](/n1/p-maintenance/tilt_the_printer.jpg) | ![](/n1/p-maintenance/apply_grease.jpg) |

### [¶](#idler-pulley) Idler pulley

This mainly refers to the idler pulley of the X-axis. Regular maintenance of the idler pulley can effectively prevent abnormal noise and abnormal resistance.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| Idler pulley | When: Every 3 months    Materials & tools: Lubricant oil,  Allen key    Solution: Lubricate    How to do: Near the X-axis end cover, drop an appropriate amount of clear oil between the belt and the idler pulley. |

![pulley_x.2.png](/n1/p-maintenance/pulley_x.2.png)

### [¶](#filament-sensor) Filament Sensor

There are several Hall sensors on the filament sensor board. The attachment of foreign matter such as material fragments and dust will cause abnormal Hall values and cause false alarms. Cleaning the filament sensor board in time can effectively solve the problem of false alarms and improve the printing experience.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| Filament sensor | When: False alarm    Materials & tools: Allen key, Air blower    Solution: Cleaning    How to do: Unscrew the 2 screws, carefully pull out the entire bottom bracket assembly, remove the 2 screws that lock the filament sensor, and clean with a blower. |

Video guide for cleaning the filament sensor board:

<https://public-cdn.bblmw.com/wiki/A1-mini/filament_sensor_maintenance.mp4>

|  |  |  |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ![](/n1/p-maintenance/filament_sensor_1.png) | ![](/n1/p-maintenance/filament_sensor2.png) | ![](/n1/p-maintenance/filament_sensor_3.png) |

Note: When disassembling, do not pull out the connecting cable too much to avoid damage and abnormal installation.

### [¶](#extruder) Extruder

After using the printer for a long time, filament debris and dust may remain inside the extruder, which can easily cause the extrusion gear to become stuck or even unable to rotate during operation. In order to ensure the normal printing of the printer, the extruder needs to be cleaned of foreign materials in time.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| Extruder unit | When: Under-extrusion, Extruder clog    Materials & tools: Tweezers,  Allen key, Air blower    Solution: Cleaning    How to do: Remove the filament pressure block, loosen the cutter lever, use tweezers to clean the internal filamentous foreign materials, use a blower to blow off the residue and dust, if necessary, take out the filament hub assembly and clean it. |

Video guide for cleaning the extruder:

<https://public-cdn.bblmw.com/wiki/A1-mini/extruder_clean.mp4>

|  |  |  |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ![](/n1/p-maintenance/extruder_1.2.png) | ![](/n1/p-maintenance/extruder_2.2.png) | ![](/n1/p-maintenance/extruder_3.2.png) |

[Extruder module installation installation tutorial](/en/a1-mini/maintenance/a1-extruder)

### [¶](#extruder-gear-assembly) Extruder Gear Assembly

When cleaning the extruder according to the above steps fails to achieve satisfactory results, you can disassemble the extruder and remove the extrusion wheel assembly for cleaning. Under normal circumstances, for printing non-carbon fiber filaments like PLA and PETG, it is advisable to clean the extruder gear every 5 spools. When printing with carbon fiber filaments, clean the extruder gear every 2 spools. If you find that the extruder gear is obviously worn, and cleaning the nozzle does not improve the extrusion, you can replace the gear assembly.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| Extruder Gear Assembly | When: Under-extrusion, Extruder clog    Materials & tools: Tweezers, Allen key, Air blower    Solution: Cleaning    How to do: When cleaning the extruder, if you find foreign materials that cannot be removed, you need to remove the extrusion front cover and take out the extrusion wheel assembly for cleaning. For specific disassembly and assembly methods, please refer to the relevant maintenance guide page. |

![](/n1/p-maintenance/extruder_gears.2.png)

[Extruder module installation installation tutorial](/en/a1-mini/maintenance/a1-extruder)

### [¶](#purge-wiper) Purge Wiper

The A1 mini's purge wiper is used to efficiently process the waste material ejected from the nozzle to ensure a continuous and smooth printing process. When the movement of the purge wiper is blocked by foreign objects or the action mechanism is deformed, the purge wiper may become stuck, causing abnormal discharging or causing the printer to lose steps. Therefore, it is necessary to promptly clean up foreign objects and replace the deformed purge wiper to improve the success rate of filament exchange and enhance the printing experience.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| Purge Wiper | When: Excess accumulation or purge wiper deformation    Materials & tools: Tweezers, Allen key, Air blower    Solution: Cleaning, Replacement    How to do: Remove the tool head from the purge wiper , use tweezers to remove excess waste, and use a blower to blow off residue and dust. |

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](/n1/p-maintenance/purge_1.2.png) | ![](/n1/p-maintenance/purge_2.2.png) |

### [¶](#fans) Fans

A1 mini is equipped with 3 fans, namely hotend cooling fan, part cooling fan and mainboard cooling fan. In order to ensure that the machine has a good cooling effect, it is necessary to check the status of the fan regularly and remove foreign objects in a timely manner.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| Part cooling fan | When: Every week    Materials & tools: Cloth,Tweezers    Solution: Cleaning    How to do: Check whether there is foreign objects or dust accumulation in the air duct and clean it. |
| Hotend cooling fan | When: Every week    Materials & tools: Cloth,Tweezers    Solution: Cleaning    How to do: Remove the hotend, check whether there is foreign objects or dust accumulation in the air duct and clean it. |
| Mainboard cooling fan | When: HMS prompts    Materials & tools: Compressed air (or high-pressure air gun), Cloth, Tweezers    Solution: Cleaning    How to do: Use compressed air jets to remove accumulated dust, or disassemble the base plate and wipe the fan clean. |

After prolonged use, the part cooling fan may accumulate a significant amount of dust inside. You can remove the component cooling fan, loosen the four screws on the back, open the lower housing, and use a brush to clean it.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](/a1/maintenance/printer/%E6%89%93%E5%BC%80.png) | ![](/a1/maintenance/printer/%E9%83%A8%E4%BB%B6%E6%95%A3%E7%83%AD.png) |

Video guide for cleaning the fans:

<https://public-cdn.bblmw.com/wiki/A1-mini/fans_clean.mp4>

|  |  |  |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ![](/n1/p-maintenance/fan1.2.png) | ![](/n1/p-maintenance/fan2.2.png) | ![](/n1/p-maintenance/fan3.png) |

## [¶](#consumables) Consumables

Refers to product parts or components that need to be replaced due to wear or consumption during normal operation of the product, such as PTFE tubes, hot ends, etc.

Such vulnerable parts need to be proactively cleaned and inspected to detect abnormalities and replace them promptly to avoid affecting normal printing.

### [¶](#silicone-sock-for-hotend) Silicone Sock for Hotend

The silicone sock for hotend helps maintain the stability of the hotend temperature. If there are signs of damage on the silicone sock, or the silicone sock cannot be fixed well on the hotend, the silicone sock should be replaced to avoid some print quality caused by temperature differences.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| Silicone Sock for Hotend | When: Damaged or unable to be installed    Materials & tools: Silicone Sock for Hotend    Solution: Replacement    How to do: When the hotend temperature is below 60 degrees, refer to the hotend replacement guide attached to the extruder for replacement. |

Note: Please be sure to install the silicone sock for hotend, or it will cause some hotend temperature warning.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](/n1/p-maintenance/silicone_rubber_1.2.png) | ![](/n1/p-maintenance/silicone_rubber_2.png) |

### [¶](#hotend) Hotend

The A1 mini hot end adopts a quick-release buckle design. In order to ensure the normal function of the hot end quick-release, and to promptly remove the residue on the surface of the hot end and the heating assembly to ensure the accuracy of the hotend temperature measurement, it needs to be removed regularly. and clean the surface of the heating element. Under normal circumstances, for printing non-carbon fiber filaments like PLA and PETG, it is advisable to clean the nozzle every 5 spools. When printing with carbon fiber filaments, clean the nozzle every 2 spools. If you find that the nozzle is obviously worn causing material leakage, and cleaning the nozzle does not improve the extrusion, please replace it with a new spare hot end.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| Hotend Heating Assembly | When: Every month    Materials & tools: Glooves,Cloth,Tweezers    Solution: Cleaning    How to do: After removing the silicone sock, clean the attachments on the hot end, then remove the hot end, use tweezers to roll up a lint-free cloth dipped in absolute alcohol, and wipe the surface of the heating assembly clean.    When: Broken structure or cable, or heating failure due to print failure or disassembling parts    Materials & tools: [New hotend heating assembly](https://us.store.bambulab.com/collections/spare-parts-for-a1-series/products/hotend-heating-assembly-a1-series)    Solution: Replacement    How to do: Refer to [Hotend Heating Assembly Replacement Guide](/en/a1-mini/maintenance/hotend-heating-assembly-replacement). |
| Hotend | When: Wear and leakage    Materials & tools: Hotend    Solution: Replacement    How to do: Refer to the hotend replacement instructions attached to the extruder for replacement. |

Note:

1. If there is residual material attached to the hotend and the hotend cannot be removed normally, you need to set the temperature to 80 degrees to soften the residue before removing it;

2. Please be sure to install the hotend silicone sock after the cleaning, or it will cause some hotend temperature warning.

Hotend Replacement Video Guide:

</n1/p-maintenance/nozzle_replacement_english.mp4>

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](/n1/maintenance/clean-hotend-assembly/%E6%93%A6%E6%8B%AD.webp) | ![](https://wiki.bambulab.com/n1/maintenance/clean-hotend-assembly/05_%E6%93%A6%E6%8B%AD%E7%83%AD%E7%AB%AF.gif) |

### [¶](#filament-cutter) Filament Cutter

Like the X-series printers, the A1 mini is also equipped with a cutter to achieve automatic filament changing. If you use multi-color printing, the cutter blade will become dull or even chipped after printing multiple rolls of filaments. Therefore, it is recommended to check regularly to ensure that the blade is still sharp and intact to avoid losing steps due to high cutting resistance and improve printing success rate. If you find that the cutter is worn out and unable to cut the filaments smoothly, please replace it with a new cutter.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| Filament cutter | When: Check the blade once every 3 rolls of PLA\ABS\PETG    Materials & tools: Allen key    Solution: Replacement    How to do: Disassemble the filament pressing block and check whether the blade is missing. If there is any abnormality, release the cutter handle and replace it with a new cutter. |

Video guide for filament cutter maintenance and replacement:

<https://public-cdn.bblmw.com/wiki/A1-mini/filament_cutter_replace.mp4>

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](/n1/p-maintenance/cutter1.2.png) | ![](/n1/p-maintenance/cutter2.2.png) |

We also have a [detailed Filament Cutter Replacement Guide](/en/a1-mini/maintenance/filament-cutter-replacement) available for this procedure.

### [¶](#ptfe-tube) PTFE Tube

We send the filament from the AMS lite (or Spool Holder) to the filament hub through a PTFE tube. After long-term friction with the filaments, the PTFE tube will wear to varying degrees and increase the filament supply resistance. In order to avoid abnormal filament transportation caused by wear of the PTFE tube, the PTFE tube should be replaced when there are obvious signs of wear.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| PTFE Tube | When: PLA and other non-carbon fiber materials, every 6 rolls; carbon fiber materials or ploywood and other high friction materials, every 2 rolls    Materials & tools: With hands or with the help of auxiliary tools    Solution: Replacement    How to do: Press the filament hub's connector and pull the material tube upward; if it is difficult to press it with bare hands, you can use tools such as printed models or tweezers.    [ptfe\_tool.3mf](/n1/p-maintenance/ptfe_tool.3mf) |

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](/n1/p-maintenance/tube1.2.png) | ![](/n1/p-maintenance/tube2.2.png) |

Note: The specifications of the PTFE tube used by A1 mini are 4mm outer diameter and 2.5mm inner diameter.

### [¶](#heatbed-nozzle-wiper) Heatbed Nozzle Wiper

The A1 mini nozzle wiper assembly actually includes two accessories, a silicone nozzle wiper and a metal plate wiper. The silicone wiper is used to clean the residual waste on the nozzle, and the metal plate wiper is used to clean the nozzle end surface to ensure it is smooth. If the nozzle wiper assembly is damaged or deformed, it can result in incomplete cleaning of residual material on the nozzle, thereby affecting print quality. You will need to replace it to ensure the nozzle cleaning effect.

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| Heatbed Nozzle Wiper | When: The silicone wiper is damaged/the stainless steel wiper is deformed    Materials & tools: Allen key    Solution: Replacement    How to do: The silicone wiper is replaced together with the base, and the metal wiper only needs to be replaced separately. |

|  |  |
| --- | --- |
| ![](/n1/p-maintenance/wipper1.2.png) | ![](/n1/p-maintenance/wipper2.2.jpg) |

We also have a [detailed Nozzle Wiper Replacement Guide](/en/a1/maintenance/nozzle-wiper-replacement) available for this procedure.

### [¶](#pei-build-plate) PEI Build Plate

A1 mini along with a textured PEI plate, which is made of PEI material sprayed on a stainless steel plate. It can be used to print various materials such as PLA/PETG without using any adhesive. In order to maintain optimal adhesion, it is recommended to clean the printing plate regularly. and please avoid direct contact of fingers with the surface of the printing plate to prevent skin oils from adhering to the surface of the printing plate and affecting the adhesion effect. Please refer to the following link for how to clean the PEI printing plate:

[Bambu Textrued PEI Plate Clean Guide](/en/filament-acc/acc/pei-plate-clean-guide)
